  <div  class="popup-content">
    
<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>
</form>   </div>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                                                                                    <div class="author">
                                                <div class="author__wrapper">
                                                    <img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/GIB-round-white.png" alt="The Darker Things" class="author__image">                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                        <div class="author__name title-h6">Andrey Zhdanov</div>
                                                        <div class="body2">Chief Malware Analyst and Threat Hunter</div>

                                                                            </div>
                                                            
                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">The Darker Things</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">BlackMatter and their victims</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">November 3, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Ransomware</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-69.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-69.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">BlackMatter</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Digital Forensics</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Incident Response</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Today, on November 3, <strong>BlackMatter gang</strong> announced it was shutting its <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/knowledge-hub/raas/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Ransomware-as-a-Service</a> program due to the &#8220;pressure from the authorities&#8221;.</p>
<p>However, it doesn&#8217;t mean that BlackMatter&#8217;s affiliates will stop malicious activity. They will most likely join other RaaS programs. In addition, this might just be an attempt to have a fresh start under a different name. Just like BlackMatter was a rebranding of DarkSide, a new successor may appear soon. Therefore, given the similarities that we <a href="/blog/blackmatter-ransomware/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">observed</a> between DarkSide and BlackMatter ransomware back in August, it&#8217;s important to be aware of the latest ransomware versions&#8217; features: malware configuration, encryption mechanisms in use etc.</p>
<p>For this purpose the experts from Group-IB&#8217;s Digital Forensics and Incident Response Team analyzed <strong>new BlackMatter samples</strong> for Windows and Linux, Andrey Zhdanov, Group-IB&#8217;s threat hunter, will share new data on his findings.</p>
<p>A US architectural firm was among the first to fall victim to BlackMatter in late July 2021. Since then, t<strong>he BlackMatter operators&#8217; appetites have grown considerably</strong>, the frequency of attacks has increased, and the threat actors seem to have been constantly improving their tools. The average ransom demand is <strong>$5.3 million</strong>, with the maximum, which the attackers demanded from Japan&#8217;s Olympus Corporation, <strong>reaching $30 million</strong>.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25527 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201680%20900'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="blackmatter ransomware in numbers" width="1680" height="900" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-10.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25527 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-10.png" alt="blackmatter ransomware in numbers" width="1680" height="900"></noscript></picture></p>
<p><strong>BlackMatter</strong> affiliates try their best to pick their victims carefully, so as not to draw too much attention, but they are not exactly succeeding. Since the first BlackMatter attacks were reported, they have received a lot of very close attention from threat researchers. And on 18 October 2021, the CISA, FBI, and NSA issued joint recommendations, naming BlackMatter ransomware responsible for attacks on U.S. critical infrastructure that had begun in July 2021. As of November 2021, the list of BlackMatter victims consists of more than 50 companies based in the US, Austria, Italy, France, Japan, and other countries.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-13.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25526 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201680%20900'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="geography of blackmatter ransomware attacks" width="1680" height="900" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-13.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25526 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-13.png" alt="geography of blackmatter ransomware attacks" width="1680" height="900"></noscript></picture></p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-14.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25524 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201680%20900'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="distribution of blackmatter ransomware attacks by industry" width="1680" height="900" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-14.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25524 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-14.png" alt="distribution of blackmatter ransomware attacks by industry" width="1680" height="900"></noscript></picture></p>
<h2>BlackMatter for Windows</h2>
<p>Depending on command line parameters, ransomware for Windows can operate in five different modes. We were able to obtain command line arguments based on analysis of their hashes.</p>
<p>&#8211;<em>path [PATH]</em> – encryption of the specified object (directory, file, network resource).</p>
<p>&#8211;<em>safe</em> – self-registration in the RunOnce key of system registry, reboot for file encryption in safe mode.</p>
<p>&#8211;<em>wall</em> – creating a BMP image with information about encryption of files and setting it as the desktop wallpaper.</p>
<p><em>[PATH]</em> – encryption of a specified directory/file.</p>
</div>
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>When other parameters are set or any parameters are absent, the system is fully encrypted according to the configuration settings. Upon completing the encryption, the ransomware creates a BMP image alerting that files have been encrypted, which it then sets as the desktop wallpaper. Starting from version 1.4, the ransomware can also print the text of the demand for ransom on the victim&#8217;s default printer.</p>
<p>When <strong>BlackMatter</strong> launches, it checks the rights of the current user and, if necessary, tries to bypass the UAC (User Account Control) through privilege escalation using the ICMLuaUtil COM interface. Also, if the appropriate flag is set in the configuration, it attempts to authenticate using the credentials contained in the configuration data.</p>
<p>Before starting the encryption, BlackMatter deletes shadow copies of partitions using WQL queries (WMI Query Language).</p>
<p>To encrypt files, BlackMatter uses the most efficient multithreading implementation based on the use of the I/O (input/output) completion port. The malware also sets the highest priority (THREAD_PRIORITY_HIGHEST) for the file enumeration and encryption streams. By default, only the first megabyte of file contents is encrypted. In earlier versions, data was encrypted using Salsa20. Apparently, the authors of BlackMatter, just like the authors of another extortionist Petya five years ago, made mistakes in the implementation of the Salsa20 algorithm. Starting from version 1.9, the contents of the files are encrypted already using a modified version of the implementation of the ChaCha20 algorithm, presumably taken from CryptoPP library. Furthermore, the ChaCha20 encryption algorithm is implemented using SSSE3 processor instructions. ChaCha20 keys are encrypted using the RSA-1024 public key. A data block with an encrypted key is appended to the end of the file. The names of the encrypted files are as follows:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">[FILENAME].[VICTIM_ID]</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p><em>FILENAME</em> – is the original name of the file.</p>
<p><em>VICTIM_ID</em> – is the victim ID generated on the basis of the string contained in the MachineGuid value of the HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Cryptography registry key.</p>
<p><strong>The BlackMatter configuration contains the names of directories, files and extensions skipped during the encryption process as lists of checksums (hashes).</strong></p>
<p>In each processed directory, the ransomware creates text files containing the demand for ransom:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">[VICTIM_ID].README.txt</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-13.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25525 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20981%20996'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="text files containing the demand for ransom" width="981" height="996" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-13.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25525 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-13.png" alt="text files containing the demand for ransom" width="981" height="996"></noscript></picture></p>
<h3>Configuration</h3>
<p>The <strong>BlackMatter</strong> configuration data for Windows is contained in a section disguised as a &#8220;.rsrc&#8221; resource section, but there are no resources in it.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-13.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25537 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20776%20353'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="BlackMatter configuration data for Windows" width="776" height="353" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-13.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25537 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-13.png" alt="BlackMatter configuration data for Windows" width="776" height="353"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>The first 64-bit number (0F8B2AB512017D0F5h) in the section represents the initial value for the pseudo-random sequence generator (random seed) used to encrypt the program data. The next 32-bit value represents the actual size of the configuration data. Prior to encryption, the configuration data was pre-compressed using the aPLib compression algorithm, which is popular among ransomware developers. Previously, this algorithm was found, for example, in such ransomware families as DarkSide, DoppelPaymer, Clop, and others.</p>
<div id="attachment_25538" style="width: 785px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25538" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25538 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20775%20352'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Configuration data after decryption and decompression." width="775" height="352" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-25538" decoding="async" class="wp-image-25538 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-10.png" alt="Configuration data after decryption and decompression." width="775" height="352"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-25538" class="wp-caption-text">Configuration data after decryption and decompression.</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;75%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="25%">Offset</th>
<th width="75%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">000h</td>
<td width="75%">RSA-1024 public key.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">080h</td>
<td width="75%">&#8216;bot-company&#8217; 16-bit Company ID</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">090h</td>
<td width="75%">AES-128 ECB key for encrypting data that is transmitted to the threat actors.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0A0h</td>
<td width="75%">Logical one-byte flags that define the ransomware settings.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%"><strong>Version below 1.9</strong></td>
<td width="75%"></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0A8h</td>
<td width="75%">Offset table of configuration parameter values.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0D0h</td>
<td width="75%">Configuration parameter values.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%"><strong>Version 1.9 and higher</strong></td>
<td width="75%"></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0A9h</td>
<td width="75%">Offset table of configuration parameter values.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0D1h</td>
<td width="75%">Checksum of the contents of the text file containing the ransom demand. This checksum is used by ransomware to avoid the encryption of its text files containing the demand for ransom.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0D5h</td>
<td width="75%">Configuration parameter values.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Logical flags that indicate the ransomware settings:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;75%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="25%">Flag index</th>
<th width="75%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0</td>
<td width="75%">Encryption of one-megabyte blocks in large files at intervals that depend on the file size</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">1</td>
<td width="75%">Attempt authentication using the credentials contained in the configuration</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">2</td>
<td width="75%">Mount partitions and encrypt files on them. Starting from version 1.4, if this flag is set, Microsoft Exchange files contained in the &#8220;%ExchangeInstallPath%\Mailbox&#8221; directory are also encrypted</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">3</td>
<td width="75%">Encrypt files on available network resources. The program also lists Active Directory computers using LDAP queries.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">4</td>
<td width="75%">Terminate processes that contain the specified substrings in their names. The list of substrings is contained in the configuration data.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">5</td>
<td width="75%">Stop and delete services. A list of service names is contained in the configuration data.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">6</td>
<td width="75%">Create and check the mutex: Global\[MUTEX_NAME] MUTEX_NAME – is the mutex name generated from the string from the MachineGuid registry parameter.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">7</td>
<td width="75%">Print the text file with the ransom demand when the encryption is complete (version 1.9 and higher).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">8</td>
<td width="75%">Transmit data about the compromised system and encryption results to threat actors. Information in encrypted form (AES-128 ECB) is sent as HTTP POST requests. The list of addresses is contained in the configuration data.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p><strong>Offset table of configuration parameter values</strong></p>
<p>The table contains 32-bit numbers that represent offsets relative to the beginning of the list itself to the rest of the configuration data fields as Base64 strings, ending with a null byte. If the offset is 0, there is no field value.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;75%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="25%">Offset</th>
<th width="75%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">00h</td>
<td width="75%">Offset the list of checksums of directory names that are skipped during encryption</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">04h</td>
<td width="75%">Offset the list of checksums of files that are skipped during encryption</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">08h</td>
<td width="75%">Offset the list of checksums of file extensions skipped during encryption</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">0Ch</td>
<td width="75%">Offset the list of checksums of computer names that are not encrypted in safe mode (version 1.9 and higher)</td>
</tr>
<tr>

</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>BlackMatter for Linux</h2>
<p><strong>BlackMatter</strong> <strong>ransomware for Linux targets VMware ESXi servers</strong>. According to the settings in the configuration data, the ransomware can stop virtual machines and terminate specified processes before data encryption. The ransomware also disables the firewall. To encrypt virtual machine files, the ransomware uses the esxcli utility to obtain a list of storages with &#8220;vmfs&#8221;, &#8220;vffs&#8221; and &#8220;nfs&#8221; file systems.</p>
<p>BlackMatter for Linux implements multithreaded file encryption with the extensions specified in the configuration. Data is encrypted in blocks that are multiples of one megabyte using the HC-256 stream encryption algorithm. HC-256 keys are encrypted using the RSA-4096 public key. The CryptoPP crypto library is used to implement encryption algorithms.</p>
<p>Data transferring to the attacker-controlled resources on the internet is implemented in the malware using the libcurl library.</p>
<h3>Configuration</h3>
<p>BlackMatter configuration data for Linux is contained in the &#8220;.cfgETD&#8221; section of the ELF file. The data is encrypted, compressed using the zlib data compression library, and encoded using Base64.</p>
<p>Encrypted configuration data after Base64 decoding and zlib decompression:</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-11.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25535 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20778%20353'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="BlackMatter configuration data for Linux" width="778" height="353" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-11.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25535 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-11.png" alt="BlackMatter configuration data for Linux" width="778" height="353"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>Configuration data is encrypted using a cyclic bytewise XOR operation using the key contained in the first 32 bytes.</p>
<p>After decryption, the configuration data is in JSON format.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25536 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20782%20942'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="the configuration data in JSON format" width="782" height="942" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-10.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-25536 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-10.png" alt="the configuration data in JSON format" width="782" height="942"></noscript></picture></p>

</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Victims and threat actors</h2>
<p>To identify its victims, BlackMatter uses a unique 16-byte identifier contained in the configuration data: company_id (Windows version) and bot-id (Linux version). For each victim, the attackers create a Tor chat room for communication. The link to this chat is specified in the text file containing the ransom demand.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25533 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201399%20997'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Tor chat room for communication blackmatter" width="1399" height="997" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-10.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-25533 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-10.png" alt="Tor chat room for communication blackmatter" width="1399" height="997"></noscript></picture></p>
<p>When the ultimatum expires, the threat actors double the ransom amount, and later publish the stolen documents after the victim refuses to pay.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-855"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-9.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" title="10-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-9.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-9.png" alt="" title="10-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" title="11-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-5.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-5.png" alt="" title="11-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text"></p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h3>YARA rules</h3>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper"><pre>
/*
BlackMatter ransomware
*/

import "elf"

rule DarkSide_BM
{
    meta:
        author = "Andrey Zhdanov"
        company = "Group-IB"
        family = "ransomware.darkside_blackmatter"
        description = "DarkSide/BlackMatter ransomware Windows payload"
        severity = 10
        score = 100

    strings:
        $h1 = { 64 A1 30 00 00 00 8B B0 A4 00 00 00 8B B8 A8 00
                00 00 83 FE 05 75 05 83 FF 01 }

    condition:
        ((uint16(0) == 0x5A4D) and (uint32(uint32(0x3C)) == 0x00004550)) and
        (
            (1 of ($h*))
        )
}

rule BlackMatter
{
    meta:
        author = "Andrey Zhdanov"
        company = "Group-IB"
        family = "ransomware.blackmatter.windows"
        description = "BlackMatter ransomware Windows payload"
        severity = 10
        score = 100

    strings:
        $h0 = { 80 C6 61 80 EE 61 C1 CA 0D 03 D0 }
        $h1 = { 02 F1 2A F1 B9 0D 00 00 00 D3 CA 03 D0 }
        $h2 = { 3C 2B 75 04 B0 78 EB 0E 3C 2F 75 04 B0 69 EB 06
                3C 3D 75 02 B0 7A }
        $h3 = { 33 C0 40 40 8D 0C C5 01 00 00 00 83 7D 0? 00 75
                04 F7 D8 EB 0? }

    condition:
        ((uint16(0) == 0x5A4D) and (uint32(uint32(0x3C)) == 0x00004550)) and
        (
            (1 of ($h*))
        )
}

rule BlackMatter_Linux
{
    meta:
        author = "Andrey Zhdanov"
        company = "Group-IB"
        family = "ransomware.blackmatter.linux"
        description = "BlackMatter ransomware Linux payload"
        severity = 10
        score = 100

    strings:
        $h0 = { 0F B6 10 84 D2 74 19 0F B6 34 0F 40 38 F2 74 10
                48 83 C1 01 31 F2 48 83 F9 20 88 10 49 0F 44 C9
                48 83 C0 01 4C 39 C0 75 D7 }
        $h1 = { 44 42 46 44 C7 4? [1-2] 30 35 35 43 C7 4? [1-2]
                2D 39 43 46 C7 4? [1-2] 32 2D 34 42 C7 4? [1-2]
                42 38 2D 39 C7 4? [1-2] 30 38 45 2D C7 4? [1-2]
                36 44 41 32 C7 4? [1-2] 32 33 32 31 C7 4? [1-2]
                42 46 31 37 }

    condition:
        (uint32(0) == 0x464C457F) and
        (
            (1 of ($h*)) or
            for any i in (0..elf.number_of_sections-2):
            (
                (elf.sections[i].name == ".app.version") and
                (elf.sections[i+1].name == ".cfgETD")
            )
        )
}
</pre></div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>How to protect your network against ransomware</h2>
<ul>
<li>Make your remote access tools secure. Use multifactor authentication or at least set complex passwords and change them regularly.</li>
<li>Eliminate vulnerabilities in publicly accessible apps as soon as possible, especially those that could allow attackers to bypass the external perimeter.</li>
<li>Implement comprehensive email protection to detect and stem the most sophisticated threats. <a href="/products/business-email-protection/">More</a></li>
<li>Monitor what your contractors do in your network. Providing them with remote access should be strictly regulated.</li>
<li>Instantly patch vulnerabilities on hosts on the internal network that attackers could leverage to escalate privileges or propagate across the network.</li>
<li>Monitor the use of dual-use tools that could help attackers conduct network reconnaissance, obtain authentication data, and much more.</li>
<li>Restrict access to cloud storage. This will help keep attackers from exfiltrating data from the corporate network.</li>
<li>Make sure all accounts have the least possible privileges on the systems. In case of an attack, this will make it difficult for threat actors to move laterally across the network.</li>
<li>Use separate accounts with multifactor authentication to access servers containing backups. Moreover, make sure that you have offline copies.</li>
<li>Implement a modern threat monitoring and blocking tool that will help contain and repel attacks at any stage of the kill chain. <a href="/products/managed-xdr/">More</a></li>
</ul>
<p>For more information about attacks using manually controlled ransomware, see the Group-IB report &#8220;Ransomware Uncovered 2021/2022&#8221;.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="promo-block"><h2 class="title-h3 mb-xs-16 mb-sm-16 mb-24">Ransomware Uncovered 2021/2022</h2><p>The well-known complete guide to the latest tactics, techniques, and procedures of ransomware operators based on MITRE ATT&CK®</p><div class="buttons-wrapper mt-md-48 mt-sm-32 mt-xs-24"><a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/research-hub/ransomware-2022/" class="button button--filled button--md "><span class="effect slide-up"><span class="effect__label">Get the report now</span><span class="effect__label" aria-hidden="true">Get the report now</span></span></a></div></div></div>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
